**And the two metrics disagree, which is this paper's recurring problem in a new place.** Delivered
service goes UP six-fold and mission success goes DOWN by half. A console statistic and a mission
statistic point opposite ways because the console's gain is bought by serving requesters that would
otherwise have gone autonomous and won anyway.

**P1 (open).** The console-metric gain from $cv > 0$ is largest at spans where the blind console is
collapsed and vanishes where it is not, because there is no cohort to break up on an uncongested
console. Nothing outside $n = 10$ has been read.
  **FALSIFIER.** A gain that is flat in span, or largest below the collapse threshold, refutes the
  desynchronization mechanism and the effect is something else.

**P2 (open, and the one that tests the mechanism rather than the pattern).** On a console that
already randomizes, or that already runs admission control, the gain from $cv > 0$ is smaller,
because the cohort it breaks up has already been broken.
  **FALSIFIER.** An equal or larger gain on top of jitter or admission control means service
  variability is not acting through the arrival stream at all.

**P3 (open).** The mission-metric LOSS is real and gated, not the small sample above.
  **FALSIFIER.** A null on mission success at tier makes this a console-statistic curiosity and it
  is reported in one sentence rather than as a finding.

**CR-47 G0 VERDICT: THE INSTRUMENT GATE PASSES, AND IT PASSES AFFIRMATIVELY.** Job 9448647, full
tier, 32 blocks by 64 replications. The buffered queue must reproduce the published reservation
model under the policies where the console never commits beyond what it can serve, `refund` and
`admit`, with `blind` excluded in advance and for a stated reason. On `served_per_step`, paired,
against the reservation arm at each span:

    admit   n= 4    6    8   10   12   16      refund  n= 4    6    8   10   12   16
    effect  -0.0012 -0.0041 -0.0039 -0.0042 -0.0035 -0.0028   -0.0010 +0.0001 -0.0013 -0.0011 -0.0013 -0.0012

Twelve of twelve contrasts are EQUIVALENT at the standing margin, with the weaker one-sided
statistic running from 5.29 to 211. Six of them have $|t|$ above 5 as differences, and three above
13: at this tier the two models are distinguishable and the distinction is immaterial, which is
exactly the pair of statements a failure-to-separate could not have made. **This is the first use of
the equivalence instrument on something load-bearing, and it is the reason the rest of CR-47 is
readable at all.** The buffer consistently delivers a hair less than the reservation model under
`admit`, which is the honest direction: a real queue admits at the head against the delay it has,
and a reservation commits at arrival against the delay it will have.

**CR-48 P1 AND CR-49 CONFIRMED AT FULL TIER INSIDE SANDTABLE.** Job 9448688, 32 blocks by 64
replications, capacity ladder from 0 to 10 plus unbounded, `served_per_step`:

    clear link (lambda 3.00, k* = 2)      n= 6      8     10     12     16
      cap 0                             0.0728 0.0861 0.0974 0.1059 0.1173
      cap 1                             0.1075 0.1247 0.1350 0.1413 0.1461
      cap 2                             0.1167 0.1316 0.1403 0.1442 0.1476   <- argmax at all five
      cap 3                             0.1017 0.0956 0.1006 0.1098 0.1299
      unbounded                         0.0956 0.0411 0.0088 0.0028 0.0023

    C3 (lambda 1.80, k* = 1)              n= 6      8     10     12     16
      cap 0                             0.0445 0.0548 0.0650 0.0727 0.0840
      cap 1                             0.0533 0.0668 0.0779 0.0851 0.0948   <- argmax at all five
      cap 2                             0.0487 0.0512 0.0496 0.0464 0.0473
      unbounded                         0.0480 0.0363 0.0121 0.0031 0.0007

Ten of ten argmaxes are $k^{\star}$. **And C3 is the cell that separates the corrected rule from the
published one.** The floor rule says 0 there; capacity 0 is gated WORSE than capacity 1 at four of
five spans, by 0.0108 to 0.0129 served per step against a floor of 0.009. So the correction term,
which came out of a mechanism I first derived against the wrong queue and then re-derived against
the right one, earns its place on a measurement rather than on the derivation.

**The size, which is what a sponsor buys.** At sixteen assets on a clear link the bound delivers 63
times the service of the unbounded console the model shipped with, and at C3, 146 times. On mission
success at eight assets it is worth 17.4 points, 79.7 percent against 62.3. The intervention is one
integer in a configuration file and needs no change to the message format, no deadline field, and no
second operator.

**A metric disagreement, reported because it is this paper's recurring subject.** On mission success
the argmax is capacity ONE at four of the five spans rather than two, so the console-throughput
optimum and the mission optimum differ by one slot. They are not materially different: capacity one
and capacity two are EQUIVALENT on success at all five spans, with the weaker one-sided statistic
from 2.03 to 13.19. The honest statement is that the plateau on the mission metric contains both,
and that where the two metrics disagree the mission metric prefers the SMALLER bound, which is the
direction the queue-only model's load analysis also pointed.

**CR-38 RIDER VERDICT: P1 REFUTED ON ITS OWN FALSIFIER, P2 HELD, AND THE SPONSOR SENTENCE IS
NARROWED.** Job 9448805, full tier, `wait_p95`, one console with the rate rule against two blind
consoles:

    rung 0   n= 8      10      12      16          C3   n= 8      10      12      16
    one    18.93   19.36   19.55   19.71            19.02   19.23   19.33   19.44
    two    13.09   16.03   19.16   19.48            16.86   17.95   18.61   19.28
    verdict LONGER  LONGER   equiv   equiv         LONGER  LONGER   equiv   equiv

Two of the six registered cells fail equivalence, which is exactly what the falsifier said would
narrow the claim, so it is narrowed. **P2, the direction limb, holds at all eight cells: where the
tails differ the single console's is the longer one**, which is the queueing reading and would have
been alarming the other way.

The magnitude matters more than the gate here, and the scale is the requester's own patience of 20
steps. At ten assets two consoles leave the ninety-fifth-percentile requester at 16.0 steps, with
four steps of slack; one console leaves it at 19.4, at the edge of giving up. The throughput
equivalence is real and the tail equivalence is not, and a console sized on the first would be
delivering the same number of decisions to a population a twentieth of which is about to stop
waiting for them.

**This is the second time in this session that a claim I had already written into the manuscript was
narrowed by a check adversarial review asked for and I had not thought to run.** The first was the
plateau. Both were riders on results that were correct as far as they went.

---

## CR-47 P1-P6 VERDICT: NEWEST-FIRST WORKS, AND THE BOUND IS THE CHEAPEST OF ITS EQUALS

Job 9448647 (full tier, 32 blocks a point) closes every limb registered in `hopper/batteries/lifo.sh`.
Read with `tools/ar_survey.py`, paired by block, gated at |t| > 1.70 with the capacity-anchored rate
floor. Contrasts are `patient_first` minus `fcfs` on the blind console unless another arm is named.

**P1 (registered as confirmation) HELD.** The contrast on `served_per_step` is +0.0788 at n=8,
+0.1232 at n=10, +0.1371 at n=12 and +0.1441 at n=16, all gated up with 32 of 32 blocks agreeing.
At n=16 the ordering rule alone lifts a console that had collapsed to 0.0023 served/step back to
0.1464, which is 98 percent of what admission control gets (0.1490) and 99 percent of what the
derived buffer bound gets (0.1476).

**P2 HELD.** Mission success moves with it: +0.1470 at n=8, +0.1265 at n=10, +0.0874 at n=12, all
gated. At n=16 the two arms are equivalent, because by then both reach the objective and the
headroom has been spent. Below n=8 the contrast is inconclusive.

**P3 FAILS ON ITS LETTER, HOLDS ON ITS SHAPE.** The registration said the sign is negative at n <= 6.
Measured: −0.0021 at n=4, −0.0040 at n=5, and **+0.0080 at n=6**, inconclusive rather than negative.
So the sign change brackets at 5 to 6 against a predicted collapse span of 6.45. The prediction is
off by one span in the direction that matters least (the crossing is early, not late), but it is off,
and P3 is recorded as failed rather than rounded into a pass. What survives is the qualitative claim
the registration was built on: below collapse, working newest-first costs a little; above it, a lot
is gained.

**P4 HELD, AND IT IS THE USEFUL ONE.** Under `admit`, the same contrast is negative at every span
(−0.0020 to −0.0079), with 32 of 32 blocks agreeing at n >= 8, and **equivalent by TOST at all seven
spans**. The direction is real and the magnitude is immaterial. So the two levers substitute: once
the console can refuse work, reordering what it accepted buys nothing and costs slightly. This is
the same result the queue-only model gave and it is what makes the requirements ladder decidable
rather than a menu.

**P5 HELD.** With the reliance loop closed (`lifo_rt` against `fifo_rt`), newest-first is the argmax
at every span and the margin widens with load: +0.0401 at n=5, +0.1053 at n=8, +0.1373 at n=12, all
gated with 32 of 32 blocks. The effect is not an artifact of operators and vehicles being decoupled.

**P6 HELD, AND IT IS THE PRICE.** Mean backlog is larger under newest-first from n=5 upward, +19.2
requests at n=8 falling to +14.3 at n=16, gated throughout. That is the tail being starved, stated
in the unit the sponsor will actually feel. The derived bound does not pay it: it caps the backlog by
construction at k* + 1.

**G2 (unregistered, and it dates the effect).** Across the comms ladder the advantage decays
monotonically: +0.1232 at C0, +0.1060 at C1, +0.0899 at C2, +0.0544 at C3, and exactly 0.0000 at C4.
At C4 nothing is serviceable inside the deadline, so there is no order in which to work an
unanswerable queue. Newest-first is a congestion remedy, not a comms remedy, and the ladder shows
where it stops being either.

**What CR-47 and CR-48 together say, which is more than either says alone.** Three levers reach
nearly the same throughput on a collapsed console: refuse work at the door (0.1490), work the queue
newest-first (0.1464), bound the queue at k* (0.1476). They are not equally priced. Admission control
needs a semantic field in the message. Newest-first needs no message change but starves the tail by
14 to 19 requests. The bound needs no message change, starves nothing, and its setting is a closed
form in quantities a program office already has. **The recommendation is the bound**, and P4 is why
it is a recommendation rather than a list: the levers substitute, so stacking them buys nothing.
The word for that relation is not "dominates", which adversarial review struck out and correctly:
the three gaps at n=16 are 0.0014, 0.0012 and 0.0026 served per step, all inside one materiality
floor of 0.0090, so they are throughput-equivalent and the ladder is a price list among equals.
Two of the prices in that list were also wrong, and are corrected below.

---

## CR-54. THE CONSOLE'S CAPACITY IS CONSERVED, AND POLICY ONLY DECIDES HOW IT IS SPENT

Registered 2026-08-23, before the run, against `hopper/batteries/capacity.sh`.

CR-47's G1 sweep carried an arm I had put in as a control and then nearly failed to read. **Random
service order recovers almost nothing.** At n=16 on a clear link, oldest-first delivers 0.0023
served/step, random 0.0100 (gated up at n=10 and n=12 but TOST-equivalent to zero at n=16),
newest-first 0.1464, the derived bound 0.1476, admission control 0.1490. Random buys about five
percent of what the working levers buy.

That kills the obvious explanation. If the mechanism were "FIFO is a rigid order and any shake-up
helps," random would work. It does not. So the mechanism is not the order, and the four results that
looked like four separate findings are one finding seen from four sides.

**The proposed mechanism is an accounting identity.** Every step the console is busy goes to exactly
one of two bins: a request someone is still holding for, or a request whose requester has already
given up and asked again. `metrics.py` counts them separately as `n_serviced` and `n_stale`, so

    served_per_step + stale_frac * requests_per_step = mu * operator_utilization

If that closes, the design problem has one degree of freedom and not four. Oldest-first maximises
the wasted fraction by construction, because the oldest request is the one most likely to have
expired. Random draws it at the population rate, which under overload is most of the queue, which is
why it barely helps. The three working levers each drive it toward zero by a different route: refuse
the work, serve the freshest, or never let the queue get deep enough for anything in it to be old.
CR-47 P4 already showed they substitute rather than stack, and this is why.

**Falsifier.** P1 is the only limb that can void the framing. If the identity misses by more than
2 x MPE at more than two cells, or if the residuals are rank-ordered by arm, then service is leaking
somewhere these two counters do not see, and the one-degree-of-freedom claim is retracted rather
than patched. P2 through P5 are open predictions about the ordering, the equivalence of the three
working levers, their disagreement on backlog, and the behaviour of the split on a degraded link.

This is registered as an identity rather than a hypothesis on purpose. An identity that closes is
not a discovery, it is a licence: it says the four levers cannot be independently optimised, and it
tells a program office which single quantity to instrument.

---

## INSTRUMENT DEFECT 7: THE ADMISSION TEST COULD SEE THE FUTURE, AND IT WAS WORTH 27 PERCENT

Found 2026-08-23 while reading the partial `st-svccv-9448848.out`, before any of it was written up.

The C2 section prints, on a console with admission control:

    arm            n=8      n=10     n=12
    admit_cv00   0.1377   0.1469   0.1505
    admit_cv50   0.1512   0.1744   0.1908

An operator whose service time has a coefficient of variation of one half, at exactly the same mean,
delivers 27 percent more service at twelve assets than a metronome. Read at face value that says
erratic operators are better operators, which is absurd, so the next step was to read the code
rather than to write the paragraph.

**Correction to this entry, made the same day, before it reached the manuscript.** The numbers above
were measured on the RESERVATION console, not the buffer one. The v1 battery wrote `$D` for its arms
and `_common.sh` defines `D` without `queue_model`, so it inherited the published default. The defect
itself is unaffected, because it exists in both paths and both were read before either was patched,
but the mechanism sentence below quotes `_drain_buffer` and the arm it was measured on was
`_direct_step`'s reservation branch. Both are given. The reservation form is:

    done = start + _service_time(op, rng)   # the realized draw
    wait = done + lat - k
    if wait <= op.patience_v[i]:  ... claim the slot ...

and the buffer form is:

    svc = _service_time(op, rng)
    reply = start + svc + lat
    if reply > deadline and op.queue_policy != "blind":  ... refuse ...

So the console knew, before committing, exactly how long this particular decision was going to take,
and it accepted precisely the ones that happened to be quick. The comment above those lines said "a
console that could forecast its own service exactly would be a stronger console", which is true, and
described the opposite of what the code did.

**Why it matters more than the arithmetic.** This paper's whole argument is a requirements ladder:
what must the console know, and what must the message carry, for each lever to work. Testing against
a realization the console has not yet experienced silently adds a requirement that is not in the
ladder and is not a message field at all. It is an OPERATOR CAPABILITY: the ability to tell a hard
request from an easy one at a glance. Recommending admission control on the strength of that number
would have been recommending a triage skill while claiming to recommend a deadline field.

**The fix is a parameter, not a correction.** `admit_foresight` now selects between the two consoles.
False, the default, tests the deadline against the mean, which is all a deadline field can give.
True tests against the realization and is the triage operator. The two are identical at
`service_cv = 0`, so every published number is untouched, and the difference between them becomes a
measurable price for a capability rather than a hidden assumption. Both paths were patched, buffer
and reserve, and the reserve path gained the branch it did not have: a request admitted on the mean
whose draw overruns is worked, its slot is spent, and its answer lands after the requester has gone.

**Genus.** Seventh instance of "a convention correctly derived for its first use case, then carried
silently into a second where it does not hold". At `service_cv = 0` drawing before testing is not a
choice at all, because there is nothing to foresee; the moment variability was added it became one,
and nothing in the code marked the transition. Three of the seven are now mine and all three were
caught by reading a number that was too good and going to the source instead of to the manuscript.

---

## FABLE ROUND 24: TWO CORRECTIONS THAT COST A PUBLISHED SENTENCE EACH

Both were confirmed against the source before being accepted, and both had already reached the
manuscript.

**1. The partition has four bins, not two, so CR-54 as registered was wrong.** A claimed slot exits
`_drain_buffer` four ways: serviced, stale, a reply the downlink dropped, and a reply that arrived
after its requester had gone. My registered identity had only the first two. Worse, `n_abandon_queue`
and `n_abandon_link` cannot be substituted for the missing bins, because each of them also
increments on paths where no slot was claimed at all: a head-of-line refusal claims nothing, and an
uplink loss never reaches the console. The identity was therefore **not auditable from the counters
as logged**, and the battery would have read a residual it could not attribute.

The reviewer also disposed of my one defence. I had argued that the identity is not a tautology
because `requests_per_step` is inflated by retries, so `stale_frac`'s denominator is a different
population. That is algebraically empty: `stale_frac * requests_per_step` is `n_stale / steps` and
`n_request` cancels. The identity is not a tautology, but for a different reason: it is a partition
whose bins were not all being counted.

`n_worked_lost` and `n_worked_late` now count only the consuming paths. Smoke-tested at n=16 on one
seed, the four bins close against `mu * utilization` to within 0.0011 in all five arms, and the
residual is the service still in progress when the mission clock stops, which is one of the three
leaks the reviewer named in advance.

**2. Mean backlog is the wrong unit for starvation, and the manuscript's claim is backwards.** I
wrote that newest-first buys its throughput by starving the tail, and priced it at a mean backlog of
146 requests against oldest-first's 132. The reviewer's objection was that 146 zombies measure
memory rather than harm, and that newest-first starves *requests* while a starved requester re-asks
and its new request lands on top of the pile it was buried under, so the *vehicles* may be fine.

They are more than fine. Measured on one seed at sixteen assets with the new `starved_frac` (asked
at least once, never once answered):

    oldest-first  0.812      newest-first  0.000
    random        0.500      bound k*=2    0.000
                             admission     0.000

and the mean per-vehicle answered-over-asked rate runs 0.009, 0.023, 0.409, 0.394, 0.395 in the same
order. **Newest-first starves nobody, and has the highest per-vehicle answer rate of the five.** It
is oldest-first that abandons four fifths of the force. The manuscript paragraph has the sign of the
harm claim inverted and will be rewritten against the full-tier battery rather than patched.

**3. The finding is utilization invariance, and that is a better sentence than any of mine.** The
five arms run the console at 0.994, 0.994, 0.994, 0.985 and 0.983 utilization. The console is flat
out under every policy. So the sixty-fold spread in delivered service is **composition, not effort**,
and no amount of working the operator harder is available as a lever. That is the one-line version
of everything CR-47 and CR-48 measured, and it is registered as P6 of the rewritten battery.

**4. Accepted without argument.** "Dominates" is struck: the three levers' gaps sit inside one
materiality floor and the relation is a price list among equals. "10 of 10 argmaxes" is inflated as
stated and needs the sharp-cell count, meaning the subset of cells whose plateau is at most two wide.
P3's one-span miss is the already-documented optimistic bias in `n*` recurring, not a new mystery,
and `k*` is separable from it because one is cycle arithmetic carrying `tau` and `d` and the other is
deadline arithmetic carrying neither.

**5. Declined, with reasons.** The reviewer's headline recommendation is a cross-tier confirmation
against ProjectGL itself, on the grounds that the sponsor's blocked decision is "may I act on this
simulator at all" and that zero cross-tier points exist. That is the right experiment and it is not
ours to run: ProjectGL is Government-furnished and not in hand. It is recorded as the top ask for the
sponsor rather than as a battery. The reviewer's own named fallback, CR-46 v2, stays in the backlog.

---

## INSTRUMENT DEFECT 8: A BATTERY ABOUT THE QUEUE THAT RAN THE CONSOLE WITHOUT ONE

Found 2026-08-23 by reading two batteries against each other rather than each on its own.

`st-svccv-9448848` (v1) and `st-lifo-9448647` each contain an arm with what should be the identical
configuration: a blind direct console on a clear link. They disagree. At eight assets the lifo
battery's `fifo` delivers 0.0411 served per step and the svccv battery's `cv00` delivers 0.0197, on
32 blocks each, so it is not noise.

`hopper/batteries/_common.sh` defines

    D='"control_mode":"direct","comms_level":0'

with no `queue_model`, so it inherits the published default, which is `reserve`. Eleven batteries use
`$D` and are right to: reserve is the published console and those batteries are about it. The svccv
battery registered CR-51 and CR-53 **against the buffer console**, wrote `$D` for its arms, and
measured reserve. Two sections were read before the discrepancy surfaced.

**Genus, and it is a new one.** The previous seven were all "a convention correctly derived for its
first use case, carried silently into a second where it does not hold". This is different: a shared
default that is correct everywhere it had been used, and that became wrong the moment a second model
existed for the same thing. Nothing about `D` changed; the world around it did. The guard is
therefore not a check but a name: `_common.sh` now also defines `DB` for the buffer console, and
carries the incident in a comment, because the failure is invisible at the call site.

**What is salvaged.** v1's C1 section is a valid measurement, of the reservation console rather than
the buffer one, and it says the same thing v2's C1 says about the buffer console: service
variability is a gated LOSS below the collapse span and irrelevant above it. On reserve, cv = 1.0
costs 0.0213 served per step at four assets and 0.0332 at six; on buffer, 0.0205 at four and 0.0318
at six. Two different consoles, same shape, same sign, nearly the same size. That is a robustness
result nobody registered and it is worth keeping.

**What this cost.** Nothing that reached the manuscript, because the battery was voided for an
unrelated reason first. It is the fourth thing wrong with that battery, after the no-op jitter arms,
the foresight defect, and the P1 registration whose letter was the reverse of the measurement. One
battery, four defects, all caught before publication, and three of the four were caught only because
something in the output looked too good or too strange to write up without reading the source.

---

## INSTRUMENT DEFECT 9: A RUNNING BATTERY READ THE SOURCE I WAS EDITING

Found 2026-08-23, immediately after defect 8, by asking what else the same afternoon's habits could
have broken.

`hopper/sync.sh` rsyncs the working tree into `/scratch/vsokolov/sandtable-ar` with `--delete`, and
`ar_job.sbatch` ran every battery with that directory as its working directory. A battery invokes
`tools/ar_eval.py` once per section, so a job in flight picked up whatever source was on scratch at
the moment each section started. Editing `c2.py` and running `./hopper/sync.sh` to launch a new
battery therefore changed the code under two batteries that were still running, twice, on jobs
9448824 and 9448688.

The job header stamps `# code=<hash>` at start, which is the right instrument and is exactly the
guarantee that was being broken: it asserted a hash the run had stopped keeping.

**Checked before fixed, which is the correct order.** Both affected jobs were re-run locally under
the current code for one arm each, at three blocks, and reproduced their pre-edit block values bit
for bit: `band_blind` at eight assets gives 0.020346, 0.020887, 0.021002 on the reservation console
and `cap_none` gives 0.038507, 0.046060, 0.038577 on the buffer one, identical to the logged values.
The two edits in flight were `admit_foresight`, which is inert at `service_cv = 0` and both jobs are,
and four counters, which are pure additions. So nothing was lost. That is luck about the content of
the edits, not a property of the setup.

**Fixed so it cannot recur.** Every job now copies `src`, `tools`, `scenarios`, `hopper` and
`.autoresearch` into `runs/$SLURM_JOB_ID/` and runs from there, so `sync.sh` cannot reach it. The
hash is stamped from the snapshot at the start and re-computed at the end, and a mismatch prints
`### CODE CHANGED MID-RUN ... DO NOT READ THIS LOG`, which is a louder failure than the silent one it
replaces. `sync.sh` excludes `runs`, and snapshots older than a week are swept.

**Why this is a defect and not an operational slip.** The whole autoresearch contract is that a
result is attributable to a named code state. The tamper guard checks the frozen scoring surface
before the first measurement; the code hash records the rest. Neither was checked again afterwards,
so a run could satisfy both at second zero and be a different experiment by section three. Two of
the three guards this project relies on were start-of-run only, and the third, the seed list, is the
only one that could not drift.

---

## CR-52 P1 AND P2 VERDICT: THE MAP MOVES THE MISSION AND NOT THE MECHANISM

Job 9448824, full tier, 32 blocks a point, three terrain profiles: `band` (the map every result in
this paper was found on), `open` (no cover at all, the negative control), and `chokepoint` (a
transverse ridge with one gap, so the map rather than the operator chooses where the force converges).

**P1 HELD, on both metrics, on both new maps.** The admit-over-blind contrast gates up from six
assets on every map. On `served_per_step` it is nearly map-invariant: at sixteen assets +0.1495 on
band, +0.1494 on choke, +0.1481 on open, a spread of 0.0014 against a materiality floor of 0.0090.
The console does not know what the ground looks like and the console-level lever does not either.

**And the unregistered half is the one worth having.** On `success` the same lever fans out sharply
with terrain difficulty. At sixteen assets it is +0.0298 on band (equivalent to zero), +0.0547 on
choke (inconclusive) and +0.1396 on open (gated up). At the collapse span it is +0.2373, +0.2671 and
+0.2769. The mechanism is headroom: blind success at sixteen assets is 0.9609 on band, so there is
almost nothing left for a console fix to win, while on open it is 0.7139 and the fix takes half of
what remains.

**The methodological consequence is general and belongs in the paper.** Measure a console lever on
the console, not on the mission, or the map decides how large the effect looks. A single-map study
on easy ground will report that a real fix does not matter, and will be wrong for a reason that has
nothing to do with the fix. This is the instrument-defect genus one level up: the metric is correct,
the inference across configurations is not.

**P2 HELD, and its error shape is map-invariant too.** The capacity ladder's argmax is $k^{\star}=2$
on all three maps at all three spans. Against a bound of one the derived bound is positive at 9 of 9
cells and TOST-equivalent at all 9, so being one short is real and immaterial everywhere. Against a
bound of four it is gated down at 9 of 9, from -0.047 to -0.075. One below is free on every map; two
above is costly on every map. The closed form carries no terrain term and does not need one.

**P3 UNTESTABLE at this resolution, and re-registered.** The sweep steps 4, 6, 8, so the blind
collapse lands between six and eight assets on all three maps and the prediction cannot be
falsified. Recorded as untestable rather than as held, and re-registered as CR-52 P3a/P3b/P3c
against `hopper/batteries/maps2.sh` (job 9449266) at one-asset resolution, with the mechanism it
needs to beat the null stated in advance: a chokepoint concentrates the force where the map chooses,
so the console should see a burstier arrival process at the same mean rate.

---

## CR-54 P1 VERDICT: THE FALSIFIER FIRES ON MY OWN ARITHMETIC, AND WHAT SURVIVES IS BETTER

Job 9449071, full tier, cancelled at its two-hour limit with all four bins complete on both rungs and
the three utilization and starvation metrics unreached (they are job 9449430).

**P1 as registered cannot be scored yet, and the reason is my error, not the data's.** The registered
identity is `served + stale*requests = mu * operator_utilization`, and I tested it against a bare
`mu`. The residual is then exactly `(u - 1) * mu`, which is large and negative wherever the console
is not saturated: -0.0328 at six assets, -0.0162 at eight, +0.0003 at sixteen. That is a measurement
of how far from saturated the console is, not of whether the bins partition its time. The falsifier
fires against a right-hand side that was never registered. Scoring waits for 9449430.

**What does not need the utilization metric is the finding, and it is clean.** The four bins sum to
the console's total effort. At sixteen assets on a clear link that total is between 0.1490 and 0.1504
across all five levers, a spread of **0.0014** against a materiality floor of 0.0090. Delivered
service over the same five spans **0.1467**, a factor of 64. The console is equally saturated under
every policy and what changes is only the composition of a fixed quantity. That is utilization
invariance measured without measuring utilization, and it is the one-line version of everything
CR-47 and CR-48 found: **no amount of working the operator harder is available as a lever.**

**P4 HELD.** The five arms disagree wildly on backlog and not at all on effort, which is the shape
P4 predicted.

**And the recommendation inverts at C3, which the clear-link measurement could not have shown.**
Against admission control, the derived bound is -0.0013 at sixteen assets on a clear link, inside
the floor and TOST-equivalent, and -0.0132 at C3, gated down, with the same gate down at twelve. The
console's own accounting gives the mechanism: at C3 the bound spends 0.0167 served per step on
requests answered after their requester had gone and admission control spends 0.0000. A length bound
is a proxy for the request's age and the proxy holds while the queue is most of the delay; as the
one-way latency grows, $2\ell$ eats the deadline and the residual the queue may consume shrinks until
only a direct deadline test can size it. The manuscript's recommendation is now conditional: static
good link, buy the counter; degrading link, the semantic field earns its cost, crossover between C2
and C3. Adversarial review predicted this inversion from the CR-50 sweep and it is now measured in
SandTable rather than in the auxiliary model.

**An unregistered bin that no lever in this paper touches.** At C3 the best console spends 0.0272
served per step, about a fifth of its total effort, on decisions it completed and whose replies the
downlink then dropped. No discipline, bound or admission rule can recover that, because the loss
happens after the console has finished. It is a communications requirement, not a teaming one, and
it is why the C3 ceiling sits where it does. Separating it out is only possible because the counters
were split after adversarial review; on the old two-counter accounting it was invisible.

---

## CR-53 v2 VERDICT: DISCERNMENT IS WORTH 2.5 TIMES CONSISTENCY, AND A COUNTER SUBSTITUTES FOR BOTH

Job 9449062, full tier, complete. All four open limbs hold, and the battery exists only because
instrument defect 7 turned a wrong number into a parameter.

**P1 HELD** (carried over from v1's refutation, and re-measured on the buffer console). On a blind
console variability is a gated LOSS below the collapse span and irrelevant above it: cv = 1.0 costs
0.0205 served per step at four assets and 0.0318 at six, and is TOST-equivalent to the metronome at
8, 10, 12 and 16. A collapsed console is spending nearly all its time on dead work and how variably
it does that cannot matter.

**P2 HELD.** On the honest admitting console, which tests the deadline against the mean, variability
is a loss at every span, because a feasibility test against the mean admits work the draw then fails
to finish in time. At sixteen assets, 0.1185 at cv = 0.5 and 0.1161 at cv = 1.0 against the
metronome's 0.1490.

**P3 HELD, and it is the result.** Foresight is worth more than variability costs, by a lot. At
sixteen assets consistency (cv 0.5 to 0, no triage) is worth +0.0305 and discernment (triage, at
cv 0.5) is worth +0.0764, a ratio of 2.5, both gated with 32 of 32 blocks. So **a discerning but
erratic operator beats a consistent but undiscerning one by +0.0459**, and the margin widens with
dispersion: at an exponential service time the triage console reaches 0.2659 while the undiscerning
one has fallen to 0.1161. Variance is an opportunity to whoever can see it and a cost to whoever
cannot, and it is the same variance in both cases.

**P4 HELD.** Discernment's value rises with span: +0.0116 at six assets, +0.0246 at eight, +0.0532 at
twelve, +0.0764 at sixteen. Triage needs something to triage away.

**P5 HELD, and it is the corollary worth acting on.** Under the derived backlog bound, discernment is
worth +0.0132 rather than +0.0764, smaller by a factor of 5.8, because a console holding $k^{\star}$
requests has almost nothing left to choose between. **The cheap structural fix substitutes for the
expensive human one.** This is the same substitution the queue discipline showed, one rung up the
ladder, and the more consequential instance: a counter in a configuration file is a procurement item
and an operator who can triage by difficulty is a selection and training programme.

**Why this is worth having and was nearly lost.** The number that exposed the defect, an admitting
console apparently delivering 27 percent more when its operator became erratic, was absurd on its
face, which is what sent me to the source instead of to the manuscript. Had it been merely
surprising rather than absurd it would have shipped. The fix was to make the hidden assumption a
parameter rather than to remove it, and the parameter turned out to price a real and separable
capability. Three of the nine instrument defects in this project were found this way and none were
found by a test.

---

## CR-52 P3 RETRACTED ON ITS OWN FALSIFIER, AND THE NEGATIVE IS THE BETTER RESULT

Job 9449266, full tier, one-asset resolution from four to ten, both consoles.

P3 said the collapse span is smaller on `chokepoint` than on `band`. The registered falsifier said:
if the half-peak span is the same on all three maps to within one sweep step, P3 is retracted and the
record says the collapse span is a console property that terrain does not reach.

**It is the same on all three maps, and not to within one step but exactly.** Delivered service peaks
at five assets on band (0.1032), choke (0.1004) and open (0.0994), and crosses half its peak between
six and seven on all three: band 0.0904 then 0.0388, choke 0.0888 then 0.0361, open 0.0801 then
0.0268. Three maps, one collapse span. **P3a RETRACTED.**

**P3b refuted, and its framing was wrong as well as its prediction.** The mechanism P3 needed was a
burstier arrival process at the same mean. The mean is not the same: `requests_per_step` at eight
assets is 0.1331 on band, 0.1305 on choke and 0.1220 on open, so the maps differ in first moment and
a second-moment story cannot be isolated from this sweep at all. Mean backlog does separate at nine
and ten assets (choke above band, gated) but with the rate moving too, that is not evidence of
clumping.

**P3c refuted with the sign wrong.** Starvation at the collapse span is LOWER on chokepoint (0.2058
of assets never once answered at eight assets) than on band (0.2581), where the prediction was
higher.

**What the battery bought instead, and it is worth more than P3 was.** First, the closed form is now
confirmed at one-asset resolution rather than bracketed at two. Equation `n* = c(P+tau+1)mu/d`
predicts 6.45 and the measurement puts the peak at five and the half-peak crossing at seven, so the
predicted threshold sits inside the interval where the fall actually happens, on every map. Second,
the negative itself: **the collapse span is a property of the console and terrain does not reach it.**
The equation has no terrain term, three maps that differ enormously in mission outcome collapse
together, and that is a stronger statement about the closed form's generality than P3 could have made
if it had held. Third, a number a sponsor can feel: on the blind buffer console the fraction of
assets that ask at least once and are never once answered runs 0.00 at six assets, 0.06 at seven,
0.26 at eight, 0.56 at nine and 0.64 at ten. Two thirds of the force unanswered, at ten assets, on
one console.

---

## CR-49 Q3-Q5 VERDICT: THE CORRECTION TERM MATTERS AND ITS VALUE DOES NOT

Job 9449271, the continuation of the timed-out 9448688, complete and with the new snapshot guard
reporting `code unchanged through the run`.

**Q3, the patience sweep, is the discriminating instrument and it goes against my own re-derivation.**
Walking the requester's patience from 12 to 45 steps walks $\lambda$ from 1.80 to 6.75. Scored by
plateau membership:

    floor(lambda) - 1        7 of 8      (misses at lambda = 1.80)
    floor(lambda + 1/3) - 1  8 of 8
    floor(lambda + 1/2) - 1  7 of 8      (misses at lambda = 4.50)

The one-half form is the one in the manuscript and the one CR-49 arrived at by re-deriving against a
deterministic server after finding the console was M/D/1. It is not the best of the three here. On
raw argmax it is the worst (4 of 8 against the uncorrected rule's 7 of 8), but argmax on a plateau up
to five capacities wide is chosen by rounding, which is the whole reason plateau membership is the
registered statistic, so the argmax count is reported and not used.

**The two scans agree once the misses are located rather than counted.** The queue-only $\lambda$ scan
ties the one-third and one-half forms at four misses each out of 201 points and therefore cannot
choose between them. But the one-third form's misses sit near $\lambda \approx 1.5$ and the one-half
form's over $\lambda \in [2.5, 5.5]$, and the second interval is this study's operating band. The
patience sweep walks straight through it and finds the miss the first scan could not weight.

**So the honest rule is $k^{\star} = \lfloor \lambda + c \rfloor - 1$ with $c$ between a third and a
half, undetermined.** What is determined, at 13 misses against 4, is that $c$ is not zero. The
manuscript now says this rather than asserting one-half, and says why it hardly matters: at the top
of the sweep the plateau is five capacities wide and the two constants differ by one.

**Q4 confirms and does not discriminate.** Sweeping the service rate instead of the deadline moves
$\lambda$ to 1.5, 3.0 and 6.0, and both corrected forms are inside the plateau at all three.

**Q5 is the requirements ladder measured directly, and it reproduces the capacity battery from a
separate job.** At sixteen assets: a deadline field 0.1490, the counter 0.1476, newest-first 0.1464,
nothing 0.0023. Two independent allocations, same five numbers.

**One instrument note, recorded rather than chased.** `ar_gate.rate_floor()` is `FULL_MPE * CAPACITY`
with `CAPACITY` a fixed 0.15, so on Q4's $\mu = 0.30$ arm the floor is half what a capacity-anchored
floor should be. That makes the plateau narrower and the test harder, so the verdict above is
conservative, but a battery that swept service rate and wanted a generous floor would be reading the
wrong number. Tenth candidate for the instrument-defect genus, and the first found by asking where
else a constant that was right once is now being carried.

## INSTRUMENT DEFECT 10: THE ON-PLATFORM FOLLOWER WAS NOT ON-PLATFORM, IT WAS OMNISCIENT

`behavior._follow` refreshed a follower's leader track with

```python
if bhv.follow_mode == "behavior" or got:
```

so the behaviour arm took the leader's true pose every step, the dead-reckoning lines in the `else`
branch were unreachable, `trk_age` was identically zero, and `track_timeout` could never fire. C5 is
registered as "on-platform dead reckoning degrades less than link-based following". What that code
would have measured is telepathy against a lossy radio, which no channel can lose to, and the
conjecture would have "held" without ever running its mechanism.

Same genus as 7 and 8: a convention that was right for its first use, here a placeholder while the
link path was being written, carried silently into a second use where it was the entire treatment.
Tenth of the genus and the sixth found by reading code that no published number depends on: nothing
in `report/` sets `follow_mode`, and the only batteries that touch this layer use `blue_behavior` of
`auto` or `teleop`, which never enter `_follow`. The defect cost nothing because it was found before
the battery, which is the first time in this ledger that has been true.

**The fix, and what it commits to.** Both arms now run the SAME estimator: refresh when the channel
delivers, dead reckon forward when it does not, hand back to waypoint navigation past
`track_timeout`, re-acquire on the next refresh. Only the channel differs. A link follower refreshes
on `comms.delivered`; a behaviour follower refreshes when the vehicle it stations on is inside
`follow_sense_range` (250 m), which no message is needed for and no jamming can reach.

That forces a second commitment, and it is the more interesting one. The channel decides WHAT can be
followed. A broadcast pose is the column leader's, so a link follower holds a STAR: every follower
stations `rank * gap` behind one vehicle. A sensor return is the vehicle immediately ahead, so a
behaviour follower holds a CHAIN. Topology is therefore not an independent modelling choice, it is a
consequence of the channel, and `follow_topology` defaults to the channel. It can still be set
explicitly, because an effect that belongs to the geometry and an effect that belongs to the channel
are different findings and a 2x2 is the only way to tell them apart.

## CR-55 (C5). THE FIRST LEVER IN THIS PAPER THAT IS NOT THE CONSOLE

Every lever the queue section priced acts on the OPERATOR: what it admits, what order it works, how
deep its backlog runs. They are one mechanism seen four ways, which is why breaking FIFO recovers
5% of what refusing stale work recovers, and why the requirements ladder collapses to a single
question about staleness. A sponsor reading only that section would conclude that the answer to a
degraded link is always a better console.

Leader-following is the counter-case, and it is the one thread with real GVSC operational history
(Brendle and Jaczkowski 2002 for the Robotic Follower ATD; Pirozzo et al. 2019 for the AMAS-into-RTK
consolidation). The lever is on the PLATFORM: where the follower gets its picture of the vehicle
ahead. Cheung, Rawashdeh and Mohammadi 2022 measured 13.33% to 86.61% reductions in PATH ERROR for a
behaviour-based convoy controller against a delayed-follower one under jamming. That licenses a
mechanism and a sign, not a mission-level magnitude, and this paper is at mission level.

Configuration. `blue_behavior="leader_follow"` with `control_mode="supervisory"`, so the console is
not the binding constraint and the ladder reaches the column through the follow channel and nothing
else. Arms are the 2x2 of channel (`follow_mode`) and geometry (`follow_topology`), with the fielded
diagonal being `link`+`star` and `behavior`+`chain`.

- **P1, Cheung's sign at mission level.** `behavior`+`chain` minus `link`+`star` in mission success
  is non-negative at every rung and GATES UP at at least one degraded rung, full tier.
- **P2, the hump.** The gap is TOST-equivalent to zero on a clear link, because a link that delivers
  needs no help, and it FALLS again at the worst rungs, because a link follower whose track has
  timed out is navigating by waypoint, which is a floor and not a collapse. Non-monotone in rung.
- **P3, the price of the chain.** The advantage SHRINKS as the column lengthens: a chain follower's
  station error is its predecessor's error plus its own, so it accumulates down the column, while a
  star follower's does not. Read as `station_err_mean` against `n_blue` on each arm.
- **P4, channel not geometry.** In the 2x2, the channel main effect exceeds the topology main
  effect at a degraded rung. If the topology carries it instead, the finding is about formation
  geometry, the Cheung framing is withdrawn, and C5 is restated as a formation result.
- **P5, and this is the one worth having: the platform lever COMPOSES.** Every console lever
  substitutes for every other, because they all buy the same thing. A platform lever buys something
  the console cannot, so at direct control the joint gain of admission control plus behaviour
  following should be the SUM of the two separately, within MPE, rather than the max.

**FALSIFIER for P1**, which is the conjecture: if `behavior`+`chain` minus `link`+`star` is negative
or immaterial at EVERY rung, C5 is retracted and the record says on-platform following does not
transfer from path error to mission outcome at this fidelity. That negative is worth as much as the
positive, because it is the boundary of what a mission-level simulator can carry from a controls
result, and this paper's own scope note already says that boundary exists.

**FALSIFIER for P5**: if the joint gain is below the sum by more than MPE, the platform lever is
buying queue relief by another route, the "not the console" framing is wrong, and the levers are one
mechanism after all.

**Registered before the battery ran.** Instrument defect 10 was fixed first and the fix is described
above; no arm here was run on the omniscient follower.

## CR-55 AMENDED BEFORE ITS BATTERY: THE FOLLOW CHANNEL HAS A THRESHOLD, AND IT IS FAR AWAY

The predictions above were written against a mechanism that turned out to be missing a variable, and
this is the amendment, made before any arm was scored and with the exploratory evidence named.

**What a scouting run at sixteen replications showed.** With the fixed estimator, the link follower
at C5 runs on a dead-reckoned track 55% of the time, and its track error against the truth is
**0.6 m**. Station error moves 12.9 to 15.8 m. Mission success does not move at all. The reason is
arithmetic, not simulation: an i.i.d. channel at drop rate $p$ produces outages of mean length
$1/(1-p)$, which is 2.2 steps at the worst rung, and a constant-velocity extrapolation of a vehicle
turning at 20 mrad per step is accurate to under a metre over two steps.

**The missing variable is therefore not the channel's RATE, it is its OUTAGE LENGTH.** Leading order,
a track $a$ steps stale, following a reference at speed $v$ turning at $\omega$ rad per step, is
wrong by about $v \omega a^2 / 2$. The square is what matters: doubling the outage quadruples the
error. Under Gilbert-Elliot at the same mean drop rate, mean outage $L$, the scouting run gives track
error 0.19 m at $L = 4$, 1.18 m at $L = 20$, 2.81 m at $L = 60$, which is the quadratic.

**And the mission's own sensitivity is separately measurable.** Sweeping `formation_spread` on the
scripted path, success runs 0.906 at 10 m, 0.781 at 40 m, 0.812 at 120 m, 0.781 at 300 m and 0.562
at 600 m, with losses 0.352 to 0.480 and time to objective 991 s to 1572 s. Mission outcome responds
to column geometry at the scale of HUNDREDS of metres. The follow channel moves geometry by ones of
metres. That gap, two orders of magnitude, is the finding, and it is a composition of two slopes
measured on different arms rather than a null someone failed to reject.

C5 restated: **the follow channel reaches the mission only through station error, and the outage
duration needed to move station error to the scale the mission responds to is about a minute of
continuous loss.** At $v = 12$, $\omega = 0.02$, reaching 300 m of error needs $a \approx 50$ steps.
An i.i.d. channel at $p = 0.55$ produces a 50-step outage with probability $0.55^{50} \approx
10^{-13}$: never, in any mission anyone will run. A bursty channel with mean bad run 60 produces one
routinely. So this is C1's claim, that mean drop rate is not a sufficient statistic, carried from the
console to the platform, and it comes with a requirement a sponsor can write down: **specify a
follow radio on its worst-case outage duration, not on its packet delivery ratio.**

Amended predictions, replacing P1 to P5 above. P4 and P5 are unchanged in intent.

- **A1, the null the mechanism predicts.** Under the Bernoulli ladder, behaviour-chain minus
  link-star in SUCCESS is TOST-equivalent to zero at every rung. FALSIFIER: any rung where it gates.
- **A2, the quadratic.** In the link arm under Gilbert-Elliot at C3 with the step clock, the
  log-log slope of `track_err_mean` against `ge_burst_len` over {4, 20, 60, 120, 240} is 2 within
  [1.5, 2.5]. FALSIFIER: a slope outside that band, which would say the estimator error is not
  dominated by the curvature term and the closed form above is wrong.
- **A3, the threshold, and the number the sponsor gets.** There is an outage length $L^*$ at which
  behaviour-chain minus link-star gates UP in success, and it is between 40 and 120 steps.
  FALSIFIER: no gate at any $L$ up to 240, which retracts the transfer entirely and leaves A1 plus
  A2 as a scope boundary: the mechanism is real, quantitative, and does not reach the mission.
- **A4, matched means.** At C3 the Bernoulli and the Gilbert-Elliot channel deliver the same mean
  rate by construction; if their follow outcomes differ, mean drop rate is not a sufficient
  statistic for the PLATFORM either. FALSIFIER: no difference at $L = 240$.
- **A5, composition.** At the burst length A3 finds, and at direct control where the console binds,
  the platform lever and the admission lever compose ADDITIVELY rather than substituting.
  FALSIFIER: joint gain below the sum by more than MPE. Conditional on A3; unrunnable if A3 fails.

**Provenance of the amendment.** The scouting numbers above are 8 to 32 replications on a laptop,
which is below proxy tier and is not evidence for anything. They are recorded because they changed
the design, and a registration that hides the pilot that shaped it is not a registration. Every
number the paper uses comes from the battery, at full tier, on the gate.

## CR-56. THE VALUE OF TRIAGE, PRICED FOR AN OPERATOR WHO IS NOT AN ORACLE

Fable's Round 25 audit of the discernment result, taken in full. The +0.0764 that `admit_foresight`
buys is the value of PERFECT triage: the console sees the exact service time before committing. No
human does. The defensible parameter is an acuity mixture, now implemented as `admit_acuity`: the
console sees the realization with probability $p$ and the mean otherwise, with the same Bayes rule
either way. It is bit-identical to the old switch at $p = 1$ and to the deadline-field console at
$p = 0$, so the measured numbers still reproduce and the interior is new.

- **B1, linearity.** Delivered service is LINEAR in $p$ across {0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.75, 1}. There is no
  threshold below which triage is worthless, which matters because the sponsor's question is
  whether PARTIAL operator skill is worth training. FALSIFIER: a fitted quadratic term significant
  against the block noise, or any non-monotone cell.
- **B2, the value-of-information scaling.** The full-triage gain equals $\mu \cdot cv$: 0.075 at
  $cv = 0.5$ against a measured 0.0764, and 0.150 at $cv = 1$ against a measured 0.1498, with the
  origin forced by the metronome identity. The registered new point is $cv = 0.25$, predicted
  0.0375. FALSIFIER: outside 2 MPE of 0.0375.
- **B3, the mislabelled arm.** The published sentence says the backlog bound substitutes for
  discernment. The arms it was measured on were `queue_capacity` PLUS `queue_policy = "admit"`, not
  the counter alone, and `admit_foresight` is bit-inert on a blind console because the deadline test
  is never reached. So the cheap-counter rung of the requirements ladder was never measured against
  triage at all. This battery measures it: bound-alone with and without foresight must be exactly
  equal, and if they are not, the inertness reasoning is wrong and something else is moving.
- **B4, the direction, which the manuscript currently has backwards.** Bound-plus-triage is bounded
  above by roughly 0.16 while triage alone reaches 0.1949, so the counter does not substitute for
  discernment, it FORECLOSES most of it. The sentence to publish is that the counter substitutes for
  the operator you did not train and handicaps the one you did. FALSIFIER: bound-plus-triage at or
  above triage alone, which would restore the published direction.
- **B5, the admissible null the recommendation has to beat.** A console that tests against a
  $q$-quantile of the service distribution rather than the mean needs no triage capability at all,
  only a number. If it recovers most of the acuity gain, no training recommendation survives.
  Predicted best $q$ in 0.6 to 0.8. FALSIFIER for the recommendation, not for the conjecture.

**Blocking dependency, already discharged.** Every cell here runs at $cv > 0$, where the four-bin
effort identity is stated in JOBS and jobs are selectable: a triaging console finishes short ones.
The busy-weighted bins landed before this battery was written, and the identity now reads on
`busy_total`, which no admission rule can select.

### CR-56 B6, ADDED BEFORE THE BATTERY: THE NULL THE DISCERNMENT CLAIM LIVES OR DIES ON

Fable's Round 25 named the quantile console as the null a training recommendation has to beat. A
pilot says the quantile console is not information-free, which makes it the wrong null to rest on:
it tests the requester's DEADLINE against a conservative budget, so it refuses exactly the requests
whose deadline is tight. That is information, cheaply obtained, and it is a rung of the ladder rather
than a control.

The information-free null is a console that refuses a fixed fraction of head-of-line requests AT
RANDOM. Same lever, fewer requests admitted; no information at all about which. Implemented as
`admit_refuse`, and it decomposes the discernment gain into a part that any tightening buys and a
part that only knowing this request's difficulty can buy.

Pilot, 16 replications at twelve assets and $cv = 0.5$, served per step: the mean-test console
0.1220, random refusal 0.1225 / 0.1246 / 0.1141 / 0.0794 at refusal rates 0.10 / 0.25 / 0.50 / 0.75,
the 0.95-quantile console 0.1399, perfect foresight 0.1740. Registered decomposition, to be scored
at full tier: **tightness alone is worth about 5% of the gain, a deadline-aware budget about a
third, and the remaining two thirds requires knowing this request's difficulty.**

FALSIFIER for the section, not just the prediction: if random refusal at its best rate recovers more
than half the foresight gain, the discernment result is an admission-rate artefact, the section comes
out of the paper, and what survives is a sentence saying admission rate was the confound.

## CR-55 A3 IS CONTAMINATED BY MY OWN TIMEOUT, AND THE SPREAD SLOPE WAS READ OFF THE WRONG SEGMENT

Recorded while 9449505 is still running, so that the prediction is corrected before its data arrives
rather than after. Two errors, both mine, both found by the reviewer.

**A3's window is not a prediction, it is a parameter.** I registered the transfer threshold at 40 to
120 steps of outage. Neither of my own laws puts it there. The quadratic $v \omega a^2 / 2$ reaches
the band where mission outcome responds only at $a \approx 200$; the measured error table, which is
LINEAR in mean bad-run rather than quadratic, needs about 600. The number 40 is `track_timeout`
echoing back at me: past it the follower stops following, so of course something changes there. A
window derived from the instrument's own cut-off cannot be evidence about the world.

**The quadratic and the measurement disagree, and the reconciliation is the interesting part.**
Pilot track error runs 0.19, 1.18 and 2.81 m at mean bad-run 4, 20 and 60, which is linear within
25% and misses the quadratic by a factor of 4 at 20 and 15 at 60. The reconciliation is that the
error is quadratic only WITHIN a turn: once the reference stops turning, the heading error freezes
and the position error accumulates linearly at $v$ times the frozen heading error. The paper's
requirement, if it has one, rides on the turn-aligned tail rather than on the mean: outages that
happen to straddle a turn are the ones that hurt, and at the timeout cap that tail reaches roughly
19 m of error against a 2.81 m mean.

**The spread slope was read off the plateau, not the operating point.** I wrote that mission outcome
responds to column geometry at the scale of hundreds of metres. The sweep says the opposite where it
matters: success falls 0.906 to 0.781 between 10 m and 40 m of commanded spread, and
`formation_spread` defaults to 30. The steep segment IS the operating point, and the flat middle of
that curve is what I described. The two-orders-of-magnitude gap that the whole transfer argument
rested on is not established.

**And station error is not the only path.** Past `track_timeout` a follower reverts to waypoint
navigation, which is a discrete route-level change that no smooth composition of two slopes can
represent. Worse for the argument and better for the paper: the reverted fleet is the scripted
control arm, which scores 0.781 to 0.906, so reversion may be entirely benign. The honest candidate
conclusion is therefore STRONGER than the one I registered: mission-null on both sides of the
threshold, with the residual places for an effect to hide being mode chatter around the timeout and
columns running in mixed modes.

**What 9449505 can and cannot settle.** A1, A2 and A4 stand: they are about the estimator and the
channel, and the estimator is measured directly. A3 is withdrawn as registered. A5 is conditional on
A3 and falls with it.

## CR-57. THE DISTURBANCE MEASURED IN ITS OWN COORDINATE

The follow question has been asked through two intermediaries: a channel that produces outages of a
distribution rather than a length, and a composition of two slopes measured on different arms. Both
are avoidable. The disturbance is TRACK AGE, so sweep track age.

`follow_outage` forces the refresh to arrive once every $a+1$ steps whatever the channel is doing, so
the track age is a sawtooth from 0 to $a$ and the mean age is $a/2$, exactly and by construction. No
channel model, no burst distribution, no composition. Sweeping it against mission outcome is the
sponsor's specification axis measured in the units the specification would be written in.

- **D1, the leak meter.** Run the end-to-end missions and compare the measured mission difference
  against the one composed from the two slopes. If they agree within MPE the composition is a valid
  shortcut and the paper may use it; if they do not, the difference is the size of every path from
  the channel to the mission that does not run through station error, which is the quantity CR-55's
  argument assumed was zero. Either answer is publishable, and the second is more useful.
- **D2, threshold or slope.** Run the sweep twice, once with `track_timeout` at its default and once
  effectively infinite. If the two curves coincide, the follow channel acts smoothly and the timeout
  is not doing the work; if they separate, the effect is the mode transition and should be reported
  as one, with the timeout named as the design parameter it is.
- **D3, the null the reviewer's reading predicts.** Mission outcome is flat in $a$ across the whole
  sweep, because a follower that cannot follow reverts to the scripted behaviour this paper measures
  everywhere else and that behaviour is not much worse. FALSIFIER for D3: any gated separation.
- **D4, where an effect would have to live.** If D3 holds on the mean, the residual is the turn
  aligned tail: outages straddling a turn. Read as `station_err_mean` against `turn_rate_mean` on
  the map axis, since the chokepoint profile turns the column most.

**The registered prediction, which is the reviewer's and not mine**, is that D3 HOLDS and the follow
channel is mission-null in this simulator at every outage length up to 320 steps. If so, CR-55's
sentence is not a requirement but a scope boundary, and the boundary is the contribution: a
mission-level model of this kind cannot carry a controls result about path error, and it can say by
how much and why.

## CR-55 A1 VERDICT: THE FOLLOW CHANNEL IS MISSION-IMMATERIAL, AND THE POINT ESTIMATE GOES THE WRONG WAY

Job 9449505, F1, full tier, 32 blocks of 64 replications, eight assets, supervisory control so the
console is not the binding constraint and the ladder reaches the column only through the follow
channel.

| arm | C0 | C1 | C2 | C3 | C4 | C5 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| link + star | 0.8872 | 0.8887 | 0.8892 | 0.8926 | 0.8960 | 0.8926 |
| behaviour + chain | 0.8740 | 0.8740 | 0.8740 | 0.8740 | 0.8740 | 0.8740 |
| link + chain | 0.8740 | 0.8726 | 0.8691 | 0.8657 | 0.8809 | 0.8750 |
| behaviour + star | 0.8594 | 0.8594 | 0.8594 | 0.8594 | 0.8594 | 0.8594 |

**A1 holds, and holds affirmatively rather than by failure to reject.** Against link-plus-star, every
other arm is TOST-EQUIVALENT at every rung, one-sided statistics 1.73 to 3.89. Nothing gates in
either direction anywhere on the ladder.

Three readings worth keeping.

**The point estimate has Cheung's sign backwards at mission level.** Behaviour-chain minus link-star
runs $-0.0132$ at C0 and grows more negative with the rung, to $-0.0220$ at C4. The behaviour arm is
constant to the last digit across all six rungs, as it must be, since its refresh consults no radio.
So the ladder does nothing to the arm that depends on it either: link-star is flat at 0.887 to 0.896
across a ladder that takes the drop rate from 0 to 0.55.

**Topology is worth more than channel here, and both are immaterial.** The channel main effect is
$-0.014$ and the topology main effect is $+0.001$, but the interaction is larger than either: under
a link, star beats chain; under on-platform sensing, chain beats star. The mechanism for the worst
cell is visible in the parameters rather than in the data: a star follower at rank seven stations
280 m behind the leader against a 250 m sense range, so the deepest vehicles in behaviour-plus-star
cannot see what they are following at all and age out to waypoint navigation.

**P4 is answered in the letter and not in the spirit.** The channel main effect does exceed the
topology main effect, which is what P4 asked, but at a magnitude the gate calls equivalent, so the
answer is not that the channel carries the effect: it is that there is no effect to carry.

## CR-56 B1 VERDICT: TRIAGE IS LINEAR IN ACUITY, AND IT COSTS THE CONSOLE NOTHING

Job 9449506, A1, full tier, buffer console, admitting, $cv = 0.5$.

Delivered service per step against acuity $p$:

| assets | $p = 0$ | 0.25 | 0.5 | 0.75 | 1 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 8 | 0.1182 | 0.1240 | 0.1303 | 0.1368 | 0.1428 |
| 12 | 0.1229 | 0.1356 | 0.1485 | 0.1620 | 0.1762 |
| 16 | 0.1185 | 0.1360 | 0.1546 | 0.1742 | 0.1949 |

**B1 HOLDS.** Every step gates up with 32 of 32 blocks agreeing, $t$ from 10.5 to 274. At sixteen
assets the increments are $+0.0175$, $+0.0186$, $+0.0196$, $+0.0207$, against a perfectly linear
$+0.0191$ each: the curve is convex and the largest deviation from the chord is 0.0021, against a
materiality floor of 0.0090. There is no threshold below which triage is worthless. A quarter of the
capability is worth a quarter of the gain, and it gates up on its own at twelve and sixteen assets.
That is the answer to the sponsor's actual question, which is whether PARTIAL operator skill is
worth training rather than whether a perfect operator would be valuable.

**And the effort identity survives the move from policy to capability, in the unit that admits it.**
Console occupancy is TOST-equivalent across every acuity at every span, one-sided statistics 11.6 to
186.6; at sixteen assets the effect is $+0.0000$ with $t = 0.01$. On the same runs, delivered service
gates up at $t = 274$. **An operator who can triage perfectly works exactly as hard as one who cannot
and delivers 65% more.** The two statistics are 0.01 and 274 on identical missions, which is the
sharpest form the paper's thesis has taken: capability changes the composition of a fixed quantity,
never the quantity.

The mechanism is measured directly and is length selection. The mean service of the decisions the
console SERVES falls monotonically with acuity, 5.538 to 5.092 steps at sixteen assets against a
population mean of 6.667. Even the console that sees nothing serves 5.538, well below population,
which is selection by attrition rather than by triage: its long draws overrun the deadline and land
in the late bin. That distinction is the reviewer's and it is the one the paper had not named.

## CR-57 D2 AND D3 VERDICT: THE ANSWER TO A DEAD FOLLOW LINK IS A TIMEOUT, NOT A RADIO

Job 9449524, G1, full tier, eight assets, supervisory control, `follow_outage` forcing one refresh
every $a+1$ steps so the track age is a sawtooth from 0 to $a$ and the channel is out of the picture.

| $a$ | 0 | 20 | 40 | 80 | 160 | 320 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| timeout 40 (the fielded design) | 0.8872 | 0.8765 | 0.8726 | 0.8643 | 0.8613 | 0.8726 |
| timeout infinite | 0.8872 | 0.8765 | 0.8726 | 0.8540 | 0.8232 | 0.7915 |

**D3 HOLDS for the designed system and fails for the undesigned one, which is the better answer.**
With the staleness cut-off in place, mission success moves by 0.026 across a sixteen-fold range of
track age, inside a materiality floor of 0.06. Remove the cut-off and it falls 0.096, and the
contrast against the cut-off arm GATES DOWN at $a = 320$ ($-0.0811$, $t = -7.68$, 27 of 32 blocks).
The two arms are identical to the last digit for $a \le 40$, as they must be, because a cut-off of
40 cannot fire before the track is 40 steps old, which is the internal check that the sweep is
measuring what it says.

**D2 is therefore answered against my reading and for the reviewer's, with a sign nobody registered.**
The timeout is doing the work, and the work it is doing is protective. A follower that keeps chasing
a dead-reckoned ghost through a five-minute outage loses 0.081 of mission success; a follower that
gives up on the track and drives its own route loses nothing measurable. Reversion is not the
failure mode the conjecture assumed it was. It is the mitigation.

**What this makes the requirement.** CR-55 was heading for a sentence about specifying a follow radio
on its worst-case outage duration. The measurement says that sentence is wrong twice over: the
outage duration is mission-immaterial out to 320 steps, and what makes it immaterial is not the radio
but the platform's own willingness to stop trusting a stale track. The requirement is on the
behaviour, it is a single scalar, and it is free. That is the same shape as every other result in
this paper: the lever is software, and it is cheaper than the hardware it replaces.

**And the two paths are now dissociated by measurement rather than by argument.** In job 9449505, the
link follower's station error rises from 12.99 m to 16.31 m across the ladder while its mission
success is TOST-equivalent at every rung. Those station errors sit INSIDE the steep segment of the
commanded-spread curve, where success falls 0.906 to 0.781 between 10 m and 40 m. So commanded
geometry and estimator error are not the same quantity, exactly as review argued, and the composition
CR-55 rested on is refuted by the arm it was supposed to predict. Geometry the planner asks for
changes the mission; geometry the estimator fails to hold does not.

## CR-56 W1 VERDICT: AN INSTRUMENT THAT ANSWERS "DOES THIS CONSOLE HAVE DIFFICULTY INFORMATION" WITH AN 85 TO 1 MARGIN

Job 9449525, W1, full tier. The registered test is the reviewer's and it replaced mine, which was
unsound: I had proposed that a console serving more while abandoning less must have information, and
per-step abandonment is a demand meter rather than a knowledge meter, because any arm that lengthens
a refused vehicle's hold lowers it mechanically. The sound test uses the busy-weighted bins directly.
Length cannot be selected without seeing length, so a console has difficulty information if and only
if the mean service of the decisions it WORKS falls below the population mean.

Mean service of decisions worked, against a population mean of 6.667 steps:

| arm | 8 assets | 12 | 16 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| capacity bound of two | 6.6833 | 6.6749 | 6.6827 |
| the coin, refusing a quarter at random | 6.6648 | 6.6771 | 6.6771 |
| the honest deadline test against the mean | 6.6800 | 6.6820 | 6.6719 |
| the 0.9-quantile budget | 6.6766 | 6.6658 | 6.6756 |
| acuity one half | 6.3378 | 6.0628 | 5.8217 |
| perfect foresight | 6.0317 | 5.5115 | 5.0923 |

**All four information-free consoles work at population, within 0.019 of it and of each other,
across every span.** Only the two acuity arms select, and they select in proportion to what they can
see. The instrument's spread among the twelve cells it should not separate is 0.0185, against a gap
of 1.5744 for the console that can see: a margin of 85 to 1.
C1, C2 and C3 all hold as registered, including the sharp one: the quantile console, which many
readers will take for a cheap triage, works at population because it selects on the requester's
SLACK and not on the decision's length. It is a rung of the requirements ladder, not a substitute
for a capability.

**C4 holds and is the sentence the paper did not have.** The honest console WORKS 6.672 and SERVES
5.538 at sixteen assets. It ends up with almost the same job mix as a console that can triage, and it
pays full service time for every long decision it then fails to deliver. The foreseeing console works
5.092 and serves 5.092: it works only what it will serve. So triage and a deadline test select nearly
the same jobs, and the difference between them is who pays for the rejects. That is a cleaner account
of the 0.0764 than "discernment is worth 2.5 times consistency", and it is measurable in one number.

**A negative worth recording against my own Q1 to review.** A pilot sweeping `track_timeout` at 32
replications under the burstiest channel this paper has, Gilbert-Elliot with mean bad run 60, gives
0.844 to 0.875 across timeouts from 2 to infinite, with station error 15.7 to 18.8 m: no interior
optimum and nothing above noise. Under the forced outage the same sweep is noise as well, 0.719 to
0.938 with no monotone trend. So the staleness cut-off is a BINARY design choice, have one or not,
and not a scalar to tune. CR-57's finding should be stated that way and not as an optimum.

## CR-56 B2 VERDICT: THE VALUE OF TRIAGE HAS A CLOSED FORM, AND IT IS THE CONSOLE'S CAPACITY TIMES ITS COEFFICIENT OF VARIATION

Job 9449506, A2, full tier, sixteen assets, buffer console, admitting. The prediction was registered
before the run with the origin forced by an identity (at $cv = 0$ the foresight switch is bit-inert,
so the gain is exactly zero and no fitting is possible there) and one point already measured.

| $cv$ | mean test | perfect triage | gain | predicted $\mu \cdot cv$ | error |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0.25 | 0.1270 | 0.1634 | +0.0364 | 0.0375 | 0.0011 |
| 0.50 | 0.1185 | 0.1949 | +0.0764 | 0.0750 | 0.0014 |
| 1.00 | 0.1161 | 0.2659 | +0.1498 | 0.1500 | 0.0002 |

**B2 HOLDS.** Three points, no fitted constant, largest error 0.0014 against a materiality floor of
0.0090. The registered new point, $cv = 0.25$, was predicted at 0.0375 and measured at 0.0364.

$$\text{value of perfect triage} = \mu \cdot cv$$

This is the fourth closed form in the paper and the first that prices a CAPABILITY rather than
locating a failure. The other three say where a console breaks; this one says what an operator is
worth, from two numbers a program can measure about its own people without running any simulator:
how fast they decide, and how variable that is. At $cv = 1$ the gain is the console's entire nominal
capacity, so an operator who can triage an exponentially variable workload is worth a second console.

**Scope condition, stated because the data states it.** The law is exact at the SATURATED operating
point. At twelve assets the same three gains are 0.0248, 0.0533 and 0.1052, and at eight assets
0.0108, 0.0246 and 0.0563, both below the law and falling with occupancy, which runs 0.991, 0.970 and
0.867. That is the same scope condition the effort-invariance result carries and for the same reason:
triage reallocates a fixed quantity, and below saturation the quantity is not fixed because the
console is waiting for work. The law is therefore a ceiling that is reached exactly when the console
is the binding constraint, which is the regime the span-of-control question lives in.

## CR-58. THE BRIDGE: DOES THE DETERMINISTIC PROBE PREDICT THE STOCHASTIC CHANNEL

Registered before the run, and the arithmetic is the reviewer's rather than mine.

CR-57 measured mission outcome against a SAWTOOTH track age: one refresh every $a+1$ steps, forced,
deterministic, produced by no channel. My own objection to it was that a five-minute total outage is
not a communications condition but a severed link, so a gated effect measured only there might be
reporting its own stimulus. The reply is that a sawtooth is a transfer-function probe and not a
claimed environment. A shaker table does not manufacture a resonance, it locates one. What makes
that more than an analogy is that the probe's response curve should PREDICT the channel's null, and
the prediction is arithmetic with no free parameter.

**The bridge.** At $a = 160$ the cut-off is worth 0.0381 of mission success, and the arm spends a
fraction 0.745 of its follower-time at an age past the 40-step cut-off, at a mean deep age of 100.
That is a cost rate of $0.0381 / 0.745 = 0.051$ per unit of deep-stale time at that depth. A
Gilbert-Elliot channel with mean bad run 60 at C3 spends $0.55 \times e^{-40/60} = 0.282$ of its time
deep, and because the bad run is memoryless its mean depth GIVEN that it is past 40 is $40 + 60 =
100$, the same depth. So the predicted cut-off gap under that channel is $0.051 \times 0.282 =
-0.014$.

- **E1.** A high-power paired contrast of cut-off against no cut-off, under Gilbert-Elliot with mean
  bad run 60 at C3, lands at $-0.014$ within 0.007. FALSIFIER: outside $[-0.021, -0.007]$, in which
  case the probe does not predict the environment, the bridge is wrong, and CR-57's effect must be
  reported as a property of its own stimulus.
- **E2.** The same contrast at mean bad run 240, where the deep fraction is $0.55 e^{-40/240} =
  0.465$ and the mean deep depth is 280. The cost rate is not constant in depth, so this is a second
  and harder test of the same bridge rather than a repeat of it.
- **E3, the control that makes E1 mean something.** Under the Bernoulli ladder at the same rung, the
  deep fraction is $0.55^{40} \approx 10^{-11}$, so the predicted gap is exactly zero and the two
  arms should be bit-identical, not merely equivalent. FALSIFIER: any difference at all, which
  would mean the cut-off is firing on something other than track age.

**Power.** The gate's materiality floor for mission success is 0.06 and the quantity here is 0.014,
so this is an ESTIMATE against a registered value and not a gate. From CR-57's own pairing, the
block standard error at 32 blocks is 0.0106, so resolving 0.007 needs about 256 blocks. The battery
runs at 256 blocks of 64 replications, which is eight times the standard tier on three cells.

**Why it is worth eight times the tier.** Every sentence the manuscript will write about the follow
stack rests on a probe that no channel produces. If the probe predicts the channel, the probe is an
instrument and the paper may use it. If it does not, one paragraph comes out and an instrument
claim comes out with it.

## CR-57 D1 VERDICT: STATION-KEEPING ERROR DOES NOT PREDICT MISSION OUTCOME, AND THE COUNTEREXAMPLE IS INSIDE ONE ARM

Job 9449524, G1 and G2, full tier, eight assets, supervisory, leader-follow, link and star. The two
sweeps differ in ONE thing: G1 disturbs the follower's ESTIMATE of where its station is, G2 moves
where the station IS. Everything else, including the metric, is identical, so the comparison needs no
composition and no second experiment.

| mean station error (m) | produced by a stale estimate | produced by a commanded spread |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 8 to 13 | 0.8872 | 0.8955 |
| 46 | | 0.8242 |
| 73 | 0.8765 | |
| 173 | | 0.4341 |
| 187 | 0.8726 | |
| 401 | 0.8540 | |
| 819 | | 0.4468 |
| 1254 | 0.7915 | |

**At 173 m of station error the mission succeeds 43% of the time and at 187 m of station error it
succeeds 87% of the time.** The difference is not the magnitude of the error, which is the same. It
is what produced it. Read the other pair the same way: 819 m of commanded spread scores 0.4468 and
1254 m of estimator error scores 0.7915, so the larger error is worth nearly twice the mission.

**The mechanism, and why this is not a curiosity.** A commanded spread of 300 m is a column DESIGNED
to be strung out: every vehicle drives to a station 300 m from the next and holds it, so the force
arrives piecemeal and fights alone. An estimator error of 300 m is a vehicle CHASING a point that is
wrong, along a trajectory that still tracks the leader's route and closes the moment the track
refreshes. Mean position error is the same number for both and describes only one of them.

**What this bounds.** Cheung, Rawashdeh and Mohammadi 2022 report path error, which is the quantity
the convoy-control literature optimises, and it is the quantity this paper cannot carry to mission
outcome. Not because the effect is small, but because the statistic is not a function of the thing:
